Amid such a deluge of disasters as have rained
upas this community at large, there would teem
to be little time to weep over individual calami
ties. There was one, however, occurring yester
day morning, over which many tears have been
tbed—t°ars of sorrow and of bitter indignation.
Colonel Thomas W. Grosvenor, tfao city Prose
cuting Attorney, was shot dead about half post
12 o’clock yesterday morning, by one ot the
members of ibe University patrol guard. The
deplorable event bos created a pro
found excitement throughout tho city.
The deceased was well known and
much esteemed by every ono, and tbo thought
left manner in which he came by fats death,
growing out of ibe present disturbed condition
of municipal affairs, has occasioned a strong
feeling regarding the manner in which onr semi
military protectors have been permitted to dis
charge their duties.
It appears that Colonel Grosvenor had been
spending the evening at the bouse of bis friend,
Mr. Justice Banyon, who. about 11 o’clock, ac
companied him to the 6tatostreet car. Betook
a Twenty-fifth street car, that being the only

near to his homo at Bryant place. From
Twenty-fifth street he wes walking quietly along
Cottage Grove avenue, and had reached the
University, which is about two blocks from his
house. It was alright moonlight evening, ond
there were no persons on tho street at that time.
As he passed the grounds of the University ono
of the patrols stationed there commanded him
to haltaad givo the countersign- Ho paid no
attention to the mandate, and walked on. when
the guard called to him again, and threatened t»
fire. Grosvenor returned a careless an
swer-such as he might have done under
ordinary circumstances, telling the patrol to
M bang away and be hanged to him." The
patrol fired. Tnc hall struck Grosvenor on the
left arm, passing through the tenth rib. through
the stomach, and lodging in the liver, lie stag
gered and fell on the sidewalk, near the corner

my wife!" Rlstrg to bis feet he ran forward n

The death cf Mr. Grosvenor la greatly de
plored by all wh.. knew him. lie waaofagen
'■r<>us, genial disposition, wbtoa brought aroni.d
him hosts of warm trioods, and thero are many
io this city to-day who will mourn over him a*
fur the lose a brother. On tbo evening before
his death be spent tbo evening ata»ng his
friends and started for home, cheerful and light
hearted. as usual- with sums kindly parting
i words on his lip. He woa the least likely man io
all the city to have excited suspicion, for his de
meanor was uniformly quiet and unpretending,
lie bad fine social qualities, and ho was dev..lea
to his wife end children. They are now left al
most helpless in tho world.
Colonel Grosvenor was boro In Utica, New
Turk, in l&l. Ue haa been a resident ol Chica
go since I&7. At the breaking out of the war bo
was practising' law in Elgin, with Ed. Joslyu.
when hejoimd the Twelfth Illinois Cavalry and
was elected captain. Ho soon ruse to the rank
of Major, and at the close of tho war was made
Colonel fay brevet for hit bravo conduct. At
Harper’s Ferry he was disabled by a bullet
wound in the right arm. In the same regiment
were General Hosbrouck Davis and
Major Hayden, both now dead, who
were Grosvenor’s mo»t intimate friends and com
panions. After tbo war ho return-d to Chicago,
-.md for a time was city editor ot the Evening
Pott. Ue was then appoint, d Cigar Inspeot-r. sv
position which he held till the clo.*o ot JouXtson'e
administration. He then resumed bis law prac
iice, aud over a year ego bo was appointed by
the Common Council as City Prosecuting Att-r
His record as a soldier waa a brilliant
one, and bis private life watt unstained
oy any impure or selfiih aims. Just before he
died he requested to bo buried with Msannto hon
ors. and the request will be compiled wltn by
Kt winning Lodge. No. oil, A. F. and A. M., <m
wnlch be was a member- Tu© funeral will taku
piaco at I o’clock to-day from bis late residence
No. IS Bryant plaoc.

honest sufferers want employment and not
aim?. Every" strong, "able-bodied, un-,
married woman asking relief, ought to-be”

should be stricken from the roll.
The conduct of n majority of the* girls at

New Steam Tiro Engines,
Two steam firo engines (rum New York city and
one from Seneca Falls, N. T., reached this city
Jesterday, to take the place of the Gaud,
antes, and Liberty, which were burned op.
They bare not been purchased, but the owners
volunteered their services until the wrecka of
the borned engines are made available for ose.
Two more engines, from Beaton, are expected
next Monday.
